#2382. Liverpool played like a team fighting for their manager, but Pierre Sage looks like a dead man walking, writes LEWIS STEELE - as Reds clinch morale-boosting win at sorry Crystal Palace

Published: 11:14 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 13:00 EST, 8 November 2026

By this time of the season, spirited performances mean little if you don't have the results to go with it.

Pierre Sage, who was sacked at Lyon two years ago, knows as much as anyone that this is a results-based business.

And he simply has not had enough results. Despite having Liverpool on the ropes at times here, Crystal Palace failed to land a knockout blow and that could be that for their under-fire manager, who looked like a dead man walking when he trudged off the pitch at full-time with a face like thunder.

Former Lens over-achiever Sage now seems set for a stuffing six weeks before Christmas and though he may curse at the small margins that denied his team a chance of a heavyweight and potential job-saving scalp here, the boss knows two wins in 10 league games is not good enough.

Liverpool, meanwhile, had the width of the crossbar and a throwback performance from goalkeeper Alisson to thank. In contrast to Sage, Andoni Iraola's side showed grit and determination to win when far from their best.

Alexis Mac Allister, Florian Wirtz and Alexander Isak all scored as the Reds put a tough week behind them that saw them lose back-to-back games for the first time in the Iraola era, which prompted a chorus of the old terrace classic 'Poetry in Motion'.

Florian Wirtz scored a rare Liverpool as they won 3-1 at Crystal Palace in the Premier League

Wirtz scored Liverpool's second on 54 minutes as they ran out as 3-1 winners at Selhurst Park

Things are looking ominous for under-pressure Palace manager Pierre Sage after another loss

This performance was hardly William Wordsworth levels of poetry and Liverpool were far from on song during large swathes of this match but when it really mattered, the attackers demonstrated just why the club spent so much money on them.

CRYSTAL PALACE (3-4-2-1): Henderson 5; Lerma 6, Richards 6, Disasi 5; Mingueza 6 (Khalali 87), Wharton 5, Timber 6 (Timber 60, 6), Mitchell 6; Kamada 6, Pino 7 (McNeil 87); Larsen 5 (Nketiah 72, 7)

Subs not used: Benitez, Canvot, Chilwell, Doucoure, Hughes

LIVERPOOL (4-2-3-1): Alisson 9; Araujo 7 (Gomez 90), Jacquet 6, Van Dijk 6, Kerkez 6; Szoboszlai 7 (Nyoni 69, 7), Mac Allister 8; Gakpo 6 (Ngumoha 76) Wirtz 8, Barcola 7 (Munoz 69, 7); Isak 7 (Koumas 90)

Subs not used: Mamardashvili, Endo, McConnell, Tsimikas

Booked: Araujo, Barcola, Munoz, Mac Allister

Scorers: Mac Allister 40, Wirtz 54, Isak 89

Mac Allister resumed his bemusing role of the team's best aerial threat despite being their smallest player, Wirtz got a goal and assist, while Isak made it eight for the season to continue his prolific streak.

Bradley Barcola, meanwhile, proved what an asset he is despite a wasteful first half.

Those attackers were worthy of praise but no one deserved plaudits more so than goalkeeper, Alisson, who was player of the match in an illustration of Palace's first-half supremacy.

'I'm very pleased with Alisson's performance,' said Iraola. 'He has commanded the box very well and in games here a lot of things are normally happening in the boxes. He has shown the level he has for us. Overall we are getting good results away from home at difficult stadiums.'

The Brazilian is in the last year of his contract but put in his best performance for 18 months, dating back to a 1-0 win at Paris Saint-Germain in the Champions League knockouts. He will not go on international duty and instead rest up after an injury-hit few years.

He denied Adam Wharton with a glancing header, thwarted Daichi Kamada with a fierce shot and then tipped round Quenten Timber's effort from distance. Palace also rattled the bar through Yeremy Pino in a first half that really should have ended up differently.

Jorgen Strand Larsen also had the ball in the net but was offside.

As it was, though, Liverpool were the team to take their chance when it came after a period of dilly-dallying and playing the wrong ball, guilty again of indecisiveness similar to the flaws shown against Arsenal last weekend and in the 3-2 defeat at Fenerbahce in midweek.

Liverpool led on 40 minutes when Alexis Mac Allister (centre left) scored from close range

The Argentine celebrates after breaking the deadlock for the Reds in the Premier League clash

Crystal Palace manager Pierre Sage is facing the sack after his side dropped to the bottom of the Premier League.

Palace have won just two of their ten league matches this season, with Sunday's 3-1 defeat to Liverpool leaving them at the foot of the league for the first time in nearly nine years.

Sage is expected to lose his job during the international break, with Sean Dyche and Thomas Frank leading the race to replace the French coach.

However, Sage believes his side have been unlucky with results this season and has requested the hierarchy remain patient.

The 47-year-old, who left RC Lens for Palace in the summer to succeed Oliver Glasner, also admitted the Palace squad needs strengthening - perhaps to absolve himself of some blame.

'You can create the links between these two things,' Sage replied when asked whether being bottom of the league heading into the international break could see him sacked.

'But a lot of times I've said we deserved better and it was the case again on Sunday.

'My job is not the most important thing, as I always said from the beginning. The most important thing is to find an efficient solution for the team and for the club.

'I know how important it is for the club to stay in the Premier League. The club just have to believe in that, and if it's not the case, they will take a decision.

'I always have confidence in myself because it's a big, big mistake to not be 100% involved in what I have to do.

'We have to adjust some positions during the winter transfer window to give more solutions and more efficiency and I think it will be okay.'

Mac Allister was the scorer as he continued a fine resurgence this season, with Dominik Szoboszlai the supplier after a week where he too had been criticised. The Hungarian whipped a cross towards Mac Allister, which was glanced past Henderson for 1-0.

The former Brighton player ran straight towards the Palace fans behind the goal and taunted the supporters that were once his rivals on the south coast.

Liverpool weathered another storm at the start of the second half and soon found themselves two up. Barcola, who had left lots to be desired in the first half, swapped wings at the break and made his first attack count.

The Frenchman put the after-burners on and motored down the pitch before feeding Wirtz, who composed himself and passed into the net for 2-0. Iraola praised the run of Isak which took him away from the ball but dragged defenders with him to create space for his colleague.

Isak squandered a one-versus-one chance not long after but was not going to miss another one minutes later, when a blisteringly quick counter-attack was started by Trey Nyoni and ended with Wirtz feeding Isak, who skipped past Chris Richards and finished calmly.

The three goals will delight the hierarchy, with all three of the £100m boys – Isak, Barcola and Wirtz – registering at least one goal or assist apiece, one each in Wirtz's case.

Alexander Isak scored the visitors' third in the 89th-minute with this right-footed finish

Eddie Nketiah pulled one back for Palace from the penalty in second-half stoppage time

This victory would not have been possible, though, without Alisson, now 34 and in the final year of his deal. For all the attacking talent at Iraola's disposal, the Basque will be delighted at his team's defensive determination when under the kosh here as Palace had 10 first-half shots.

Iraola knows his team has flaws and they remain a work-in-progress. But despite a few challenging afternoons, they appear to be heading in the right direction.

The same cannot be said for Sage, whose side pulled one back in stoppage time through an Eddie Nketiah penalty after Milos Kerkez committed a careless foul.

Palace were unlucky in this game but the cold, hard truth is that the recent FA Cup and Europa Conference League triumphs feel a world away now as they enter the international break rock bottom of the Premier League.

West Ham also won a European trophy in the last five years and look where they ended up — that should set alarm bells ringing that no success lasts for ever and Palace are not too big for relegation.

Nketiah's goal felt like it was too little, too late and the sage advice for Palace supremo Steve Parish might be a reluctant change in the dugout.

#2380. Winning is the only thing that matters to Rangers midfielder Dan Neil as Ibrox club continue to gain momentum in title challenge

Published: 13:52 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 14:02 EST, 8 November 2026

Rangers midfielder Dan Neil admits he couldn’t care less about how his side go about picking up points as long as the wins keep coming.

The stand-in captain netted the crucial second in Saturday’s hard-fought victory over St Mirren in Paisley after Ryan Naderi had given the visitors a first-half lead from the penalty spot.

It was a result which extended Rangers’ unbeaten run to 12 in all competitions as they continue to build momentum following an unconvincing start to the campaign, and Neil insists that’s the only thing that matters at this stage.

He said: ‘I think every good team just finds a way to win.

‘Obviously, we’d like to win by four or five goals every single week but unfortunately that’s just not football. I do think it’s an area that we can improve on.

‘There’s been too many games this year where maybe we don’t get that second goal and it’s a bit nervy. Teams get a little bit of confidence off that and the momentum shifts.

Dan Neil savours the moment after putting the seal on Rangers' win with the second goal

‘I think it’s definitely something that we can improve on, putting teams to bed when we do go 1-0 up and go 1-0 up early doors when we’re creating chances.

‘But at the same time, finding a way to win is the most important way and whether that’s 1-0 or 5-0, at the end of the day it’s three points.’

While Rangers weren’t quite at their best in Paisley, they did more than enough to halt their poor recent run away from home.

Back-to-back 2-2 draws at Dundee and Hearts last month saw valuable points dropped in what is shaping up to be a tight title race, and Neil was delighted to get back to winning ways ahead of the international break.

‘The last two away results and performances really haven’t been us,’ said the former Sunderland man. ‘It was important that we didn’t have another one of them going into the break and have it lingering over us. Everyone played their part in making it a comfortable result.

‘Coming away from home in any league is tough, but especially this league. It’s important that the mentality of the group is right, which it is, and I think you’ve seen that on Saturday night.

‘It was important to put that hoodoo of those two results behind us and make sure that we win the game. I think there was some good football played for the majority of the game and we also dug in when we had to. That’s credit to every single player in that dressing room.’

#2378. PSV edged out by sharper Club Brugge in Champions League defeat

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

PSV Eindhoven endured a frustrating night in the Champions League as their defensive frailties were exposed in a 2-1 defeat by Club Brugge.

PSV started full of attacking intent and should have taken an early lead when Ivan Perisic put Sergiño Dest clean through on goal, but the defender’s shot was too close to goalkeeper Yann Sommer.

Instead it was the visitors who took the lead with their first real attack when Nicolo Tresoldi headed in a teasing cross from Joaquin Seys.

PSV equalised early in the second half as an outstanding pass by Sven Mijnans into the path Lutsharel Geertruida allowed the defender to charge to the byline. His pullback found its way to Kodai Sano, whose shot took a deflection off Seys into the net.

But five minutes later PSV were caught out as another razor-sharp pass by Seys released Carlos Forbs in space to net the winner.

The home side refused to give up, but were unable to find a way past Sommer in the closing stages as the Belgian side closed out the win.

Team captain Mauro Junior admitted that PSV had been undone by shortcomings at the back. “We had a lot of opportunities in the first half but we didn’t score,” he told Ziggo Sport.

“We want to attack and score goals, but we weren’ well organised today in defence. We need to keep our heads up and look forward to the next game.”

The result leaves PSV in 24th place, just above the elimination line, ahead of their next match away to Real Madrid on November 24, with four points from their opening four matches.

#2375. South Korean star Kim Min-su has no goals and no assists, but it's no problem for Rangers boss Derek McInnes

Published: 14:24 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 14:24 EST, 8 November 2026

Kim Min-su’s stat sheet since making the big-money move to Rangers over the summer should be the kind of thing that makes a manager legitimately wonder if both he and the club have been taken for a ride.

Twelve games, zero goals, zero assists. A rank-rotten return, on the face of it. So why does Derek McInnes not seem even remotely concerned by the whole palaver?

Well, simply put, he has a set of eyes. He, like everyone else, can clearly see that the South Korean is the heartbeat of everything that has been good about Rangers in an attacking sense ever since he was thrown into the team – even if the numbers don’t quite back it up.

It was the same story in yesterday’s hard-fought – but deserved – 2-0 win away at St Mirren.

It was hard to keep track of the number of times Min-su drifted in from the left-hand side to pick up possession with no opposition player within five yards. That’s not luck, it’s intelligence.

There is nobody playing in Scotland’s top flight more adept at taking the ball on the turn and driving into space either.

South Korean Kim Min-su contributes to all that is good about Rangers' attacking work

Min-su made life difficult for St Mirren during Rangers' 2-0 victory over the Paisley side

Dan Neil scores Rangers' second in a win that keeps them within four points of leaders Celtic

St Mirren couldn’t get near him for the majority of the first hour. They aren’t the first side to experience such difficulties, and they won’t be the last.

In full flight, he is a joy to watch operating in that pocket between the opposition midfield and defence.

It’s the bit after that where things start to become a little trickier for the talented 20-year-old.

He squandered a couple of opportunities to open his account here. The first came after playing a neat one-two with James Penrice late in the first half, with the ball sitting up perfectly for him ten yards out to smash home.

The choice to take one touch too many allowed Declan John to clear the danger.

So much of his game is based on instinct. Indeed, it’s a big part of what makes him such captivating viewing. But his decision-making in front of goal needs a lot of work.

Perhaps he’s guilty of overthinking things too much.

His manager certainly thinks so, admitting post-match that he felt Min-su was becoming ‘obsessed’ with registering his first goal for the club. In key moments, that shows.

A heavy touch early in the second period after being played in by Badredine Bouanani – unusually quiet here on the right – once again suggested he is feeling the pressure.

But let’s not forget, he’s essentially a kid trying to find his feet in a new country, and in a league which couldn’t be more different in terms of speed and style from what he was used to in Spain.

That he has actually managed to settle in so seamlessly should be applauded. It can’t have been an easy transition but he has quickly endeared himself to the supporters.

It’s why they are every bit as desperate as the player himself to get the monkey off his back.

They needn’t worry. If he keeps doing what he’s doing, the goals will come. Of that there is no doubt.

Some context has to be attached to his failure to notch an assist too. It’s a misleading stat, given that it depends entirely on someone else putting the ball away when it counts.

Min-su created multiple chances for his team-mates in Paisley, the best of which saw Kevin Kelsy put through on goal. It’s hardly his fault that the big man fluffed his lines. The Venezuelan has been in good form but had a stinker here.

At some point, everything will come together for Min-su. And it’s not as if he didn’t have an impact in both of Rangers’ goals against St Mirren.

It may have gone unnoticed amongst everything else, but he absolutely busted a gut to get himself into the box in the build-up to Dan Neil’s deflected strike which sealed the points on 61 minutes. That willingness to put the hard yards in off the ball is every bit as impressive as his ability on it.

And it was his pass to Penrice which led to the full-back being clipped by Samuel Ramos inside the box to give Rangers the opportunity to go ahead from the penalty spot early in the first half.

In truth, they may have been a little fortunate to do so. Put it this way, had referee Nick Walsh not given it, there’s not a chance in hell that VAR would have got involved.

Ryan Naderi stepped up to slot into the bottom corner to take his tally to nine for the season in all competitions.

It says a lot about where Naderi's head is that he had the confidence to do so, especially after blowing a great chance from six yards out only moments before. The German-born Czech international is a changed man, all right.

Speaking of confidence, there is no shortage of it at the other end of the park either. At times, the defensive pairing of Olwethu Makhanya and Emmanuel Fernandez look like they’re playing five-a-sides.

It’s usually the latter who throws in the odd lapse of concentration to add some peril to proceedings, but yesterday it was the normally dependable Makhanya’s turn to give his manager a few hairy moments. Thankfully for the South African, St Mirren couldn’t take advantage.

Craig McLeish’s men could have no complaints over the final result but could have made things interesting late on had either Eseosa Sule or Calvin Ramsay finished from close range.

It’s now just one win in nine for the Buddies, who – much like last season – just can’t seem to put the ball in the back of the net consistently. That problem plagued them so much that they ended up in the relegation play-off spot last year.

McLeish has done an excellent job since taking over from Stephen Robinson. A far better one than anyone could have possibly predicted. He has changed the style of this team completely in a very short space of time and so you have to trust he will be able to find a solution to their goalscoring issues too.

Rangers might be 13 points better off but they head into the international break also in search of that perfect balance.

When they’re on it, they’re very good. But they are still not commanding games from start to finish with any sort of regularity.

They won’t necessarily have to be pleasing on the eye to end up with their hands on the Premiership trophy come May. Celtic’s triumph last season proved that.

The Rangers supporters will gladly put up with imperfect displays for the next six months if that’s going to be the end result.

And if the likes of Min-su can hit the goal trail sometime soon, there’s a decent chance that’s exactly how things will play out.

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 11 of the Eredivisie ...

Is AZ Alkmaar's academy now the best in the Netherlands? ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 4 of the Eredivisie ...

It's time for our BIG Dutch Eredivisie season preview! Football-Oranje's ...

A new season of Dutch football is here! Football-Oranje's Michael ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul react to the Netherlands' Nations League ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul preview the Netherlands' Nations League game ...

An injury-hit Netherlands side battled back to draw 2-2 against Greece in the Nations League.

Xavi was forced into changes with Cody Gakpo and Jan Paul van Hecke dropping out of the squad. Tijjani Reijnders and Ryan Gravenberch also dropped to the bench with Quinten Timber, Guus Til, Jurrien Timber and Ruben van Bommel starting.

Greece have been in good form recently and their confidence was clear from the start. They had a goal disallowed for offside before Fotis Ioannidis made it 1-0 after 23 minutes.

The Netherlands offered little going forward and in stoppage time, a bad back pass from Van Bommel led to Giorgos Masouras sliding in a second.

Xavi made a triple change as Gravenberch, Noa Lang and Reijnders came on and they all made a big difference as the Netherlands dominated the second half.

Virgil van Dijk headed one back before Denzel Dumfries had two goals disallowed for offside. Xavi turned to Joshua Zirkzee and debutante Gjivai Zechiel in hope of an equaliser.

The equaliser came in the last minute as Zechiel’s overhead kick dropped into the path of Reijnders who blasted the ball in. Zechiel then came close to a winner but his header was straight at the goalkeeper.

A battling point for the Netherlands, who stay unbeaten under Xavi with a game at home to Serbia to come on Sunday.

The Netherlands came back from 2-0 down to earn a 2-2 draw against Greece in the Nations League. Here is our player ratings for the Netherlands players.

Bart Verbruggen 5.5/10: Wasn’t happy with his defence in the first half. Could have done more for the first goal but did make a good save. Had nothing to do after the break.

Denzel Dumfries 5/10: Struggled in the first half before improving in the second. Had two goals disallowed for offside.

Micky van de Ven 5/10: His pace makes up for the gaps he leaves at times and in the firs half he struggled. Like others he got better in the second but had less to do.

Jurrien Timber 4/10: Looked very much like a player who is not fully fit. Second to everything and gave away fouls in the first half. Was too slow too react for the second goal.

Virgil van Dijk 6/10: Defensively he struggled in the first but his goal got his side back into the game after the break.

Joey Veerman 6.5/10: Always looking to make something happen going forward. Not everything worked but was always available. His corner led to a goal an he almost scored a free kick.

Quinten Timber 4/10: Disastrous display as he gave away foul after foul in the first half. Was rightly hooked at half time.

Guus Til 4/10: Like Timber, he offered nothing in the first half and was rightly taken off. Xavi wanted to give him a chance but he didn’t take it.

Crysencio Summerville 6/10: Was Netherlands only outlet going forward in the first half and is a constant threat on the right hand side.

Ruben van Bommel 4.5/10: Was too static at times and his error led to the second goal. Taken off at the break.

Mexx Meerdink 5/10: Tried but was isolated at times. Had no service in the first half and barely touched the ball in the second.

Noa Lang 7/10: Offered more on the left than Van Bommel as he took the game to the Greek defence. His cross led to the equaliser. Likely to start against Serbia after this display.

Tijjani Reijnders 6.5/10: Came on and scored the equaliser with a good finish. Did a lot more running than Til did and it was much needed energy.

Ryan Gravenberch 6.5/10: Immediately helped the Netherlands midfield gain control back in the second half.

Joshua Zirkzee N/A: Not on the pitch long enough for a review

Gjivai Zechiel N/A: Not on the pitch long enough for a review

#2370. Dortmund unlikely to pursue Gladbach gem Mohya as focus is placed on Albert

Borussia Dortmund are not expected to intensify their efforts to sign Wael Mohya, according to BILD podcast Dortmund Insider.

While BVB considers the Borussia Mönchengladbach talent a “super player”, signing Moyha is not an option as it stands.

The reason for this is that the Westphalians have a talented player in their ranks in Mathis Albert, whose playing style is very similar.

Albert, a US international, is considered to have "incredibly high potential" within the club, even if he hasn't been getting as much playing time.

Sky had linked Mohya with Borussia Dortmund at the end of October, but now it appears certain that BVB will not pursue a transfer.

For Mohya, whose contract at Gladbach runs until 2028, numerous other options like Bayern Munich and Bayer Leverkusen remain.

#2366. Reims Fofana-dépendant

Le choc programmé dans 8 jours à Auguste-Delaune lors de la 13e journée de Ligue 2 opposera les deux meilleures attaques du championnat. N'ayant claqué qu'un pion de moins que l'impressionnante armada stéphanoise (24 contre 25), les Rémois le doivent en grande partie à leur milieu offensif Yaya Fofana, meilleur buteur du championnat (10 réalisations) devant le géant sénégalais messin Pape Moussa Fall (8), Augustine Boakye et Irvin Cardona (6) se partageant la 3e marche du podium.

Si les Marnais ont été défaits pour la première fois de la saison le week-end dernier à Grenoble, le natif de Yopougon a réalisé au stade des Alpes son deuxième doublé de la saison après celui réussi contre Guingamp. "On perd avec 70% de possession, on est un peu stériles et il y a de la dépendance vis-à-vis de Yaya. On ne peut pas toujours espérer qu'il nous sorte de moments de difficulté" a déclaré Nicolas Usaï au sujet d'un joueur appelé pour la première fois en sélection mais resté hier sur le banc des Eléphants, victorieux 2 -1 contre la Gambie.

#2365. Zirkzee fires Netherlands to win over Serbia

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 11 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 10 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 9 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 8 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 6 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 5 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 4 of the Eredivisie ...

It's time for our BIG Dutch Eredivisie season preview! Football-Oranje's ...

A new season of Dutch football is here! Football-Oranje's Michael ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul preview the Netherlands' Nations League game ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 11 of the Eredivisie ...

Netherlands edged out Serbia 2-1 in Eindhoven to move top of their Nations League group.

Xavi decided to name Teun Koopmeiners in the starting eleven but at centre-back alongside Virgil van Dijk, who was earning his 100th cap. Ryan Gravenberch, Tijjani Reijnders and Noa Lang all came into the eleven.

Netherlands made a strong start and missed two good chances to take the lead within the first two minutes. They then dominated possession but it took until the 31st minute for Oranje to take the lead. Koopmeiners free kick evaded everyone and nestled in the corner.

Oranje were only ahead for four minutes as Mihaijo Cvetkovic was allowed far too much space in the Netherlands box to loop in a header. It could have been worse for the Netherlands before the break but Pavlovic squandered a chance when in on goal.

Xavi brought on Joshua Zirkzee for Mexx Meerdink at the break but the best chances early in the second half were for Serbia. Cvetkovic should have put the visitors ahead.

Xavi turned to the bench again with Gjivai Zechiel, Ruben van Bommel and Jorrel Hato all appearing. Reijnders should have made it 2-1 but when through on goal he scooped the ball over the bar.

Netherlands retook the lead after 72 minutes with Denzel Dumfries crossing for Reijnders to square it to an unmarked Zirkzee, who tapped in. It should have been 3-1 straight away but Reijnders was denied through on goal.

The Netherlands sat back and that allowed Serbia to put on pressure. That pressure increased when Micky van de Ven was forced off and Oranje played the last five minutes with only ten men.

Bart Verbruggen made a big save to deny Nemanja Gudelj and Netherlands managed to hold on for the three points.

Netherlands first four games under Xavi see them win two and draw two. They will finish the Nations League at home to Germany and then away to Greece.

FT Netherlands 2-1 SerbiaIt’s three points but far from convincing in the end. Xavi remains unbeaten in his first four games in charge

#2364. PSV back on top of Eredivisie as Feyenoord slump in Heerenveen

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

PSV returned to the top of the Eredivisie with a thumping 5-2 win over Cambuur Leeuwarden as previous leaders Feyenoord lost a seven-goal thriller against the other Frisian club, Heerenveen.

It took just 46 seconds for PSV to get off the mark as Sven Mijnans headed in unmarked.

The midfielder added a second after 15 minutes and completed his hat-trick with a free-kick in the 80th minute to make the score 4-2, just as Cambuur were threatening a comeback.

Feyenoord’s dip in form continued as they let a two-goal lead slip at Heerenveen. The Rotterdammers looked to be in control going into the break with a trademark drilled shot by Anis Hadj Moussa and a penalty by Nacho Ferri.

But Maas Willemsen bundled in the ball from a corner on the stroke of half-time, before Ringo Meerveld came on for the second half and scored a hat-trick as Feyenoord’s defence crumbled under pressure.

“In the matches when we played well I saw a team that fought for each other,” Feyenoord coach Giovanni van Bronckhorst said after the game. “Mentally strong, kept running even when we didn’t have the ball. That’s our strength. When we don’t do that we’re a lesser team.”

Mexx Meerdink scored a hotly contested late penalty to settle a tempestuous game in Alkmaar as AZ came back from 3-1 down against Groningen to claim their first win in five matches.

Goalkeeper Etienne Vaessen was given a second yellow card for protesting too hard against the decision to penalise him for upending Ayoub Oufkir. Once the commotion died down Meerdink, who had missed from the spot against Vaessen earlier in the second half, made no mistake against substitute goalkeeper Lovro Stubljar.

Ajax missed the chance to take over top spot at Twente as their former striker Wout Weghorst scored a last-minute penalty to rescue a point for the home side.

Ajax had been 2-0 up with a bullet header by their giant striker Tolu Arokodare and a long-range strike by Rayane Bounida, before an own goal by Dies Janse brought the visitors back into the game.

Fortuna Sittard also had to settle for a point against Telstar after going 2-0 up thanks to a bizarre own goal by Sem Valk. The defender’s sliced clearance looped over the head of Ronald Koeman Junior as the goalkeeper tried to head it away rather than risk being penalised for handling a backpass.

Goals just before half-time by Abdelnour Soualhia and Patrick Brouwer brought Telstar back into the game, but neither side was able to find a winner after the break.

That result allowed ADO, under the guidance of interim coach Levi Schwiebbe, to close the gap on 16th-placed Telstar to one point with a 3-1 win against Sparta, while bottom Willem II collected their third point of the season at home to PEC Zwolle.

#2363. Celtic 1 Kilmarnock 0: Liam Scales scores only goal as Premiership leaders make hard work of it

By JOHN MCGARRY, SCOTTISH CHIEF SPORTS FEATURE WRITER

Published: 15:06 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 15:06 EST, 8 November 2026

For Martin O’Neill, the second international break of the season should be somewhat less fretful than the first.

Seven weeks ago, with a home defeat to Ferencvaros coming in between two no-shows against Rangers, the veteran wondered aloud if it might be time to ‘wrap it up’.

O’Neill later admitted that he regretted his choice of words. He won’t, though, lament the decision to go again as he rests up in the coming days.

While the intervening weeks have often been a bumpy ride, the past four days have been a lot more like it.

Thursday’s dramatic win over Besiktas has breathed new life into Celtic’s Europa League campaign.

While by no means spectacular, this narrow victory over Kilmarnock re-established their four-point lead at the top of the Premiership and will have further dampened the sense of hysteria which was threatening to engulf the entire club.

Celtic defender Liam Scales loops the ball over Kilmarnock's goalkeeper and into the net

Scales celebrates with Camilo Duran after netting the only goal of the game at Celtic Park

Celtic striker Kasper Hogh finds the net with a crisp right-foot shot, only to have it disallowed

Celtic certainly made hard work of this. Truth be told, they should have gone into the break on the back of a far more convincing victory.

While they were right to feel wronged that Kasper Hogh saw his goalless run stretch to 16 matches after having one harshly disallowed, O’Neill’s men did not help themselves either.

Coming after Liam Scales had given them a half-time advantage, Benjamin Nygren’s missed penalty allowed Kilmarnock to keep believing right to the end.

Nonetheless, it was hard to begrudge Celtic their victory over the piece. A few weeks back, this might have been a contest in which they’d have come a cropper. They look more assured defensively than earlier in the season.

You don’t need to stay up all night researching exactly why Kilmarnock are in such peril at this stage of the season.

Neil McCann’s side began the campaign losing goals and they’ve really never stopped. They cough up more chances than any side in the top division. When it comes to shooting themselves in the foot, they’re in a league of their own.

In 13 games to date, they’ve shipped 29 goals and have kept just two clean sheets in the process. St Mirren, the side with the second worst record in the Premiership, have lost 22.

As worrying as those statistics and the Premiership table undoubtedly are, though, this felt like a step in the right direction.

In being more resolute, McCann’s men stayed in the contest right until the final whistle. They fashioned a number of decent chances throughout. There was plenty of encouragement here, even though they went down again.

For the home supporters, the much-needed positivity which had emanated from Thursday’s European win was boosted by the return to the starting line-up of Jota for the first time since April 2025.

Notably, only three out of nine of Celtic’s summer signings made the squad, with Sam Johnstone the only one involved who arrived on deadline day.

O’Neill was indebted to the on-loan Wolves man inside the first minute. Ieuan Owen skipped away from Callum McGregor then Colby Donovan down the left and put in a dangerous cross. Twice Tom Lowery hit the target. Twice the Celtic keeper stood up tall.

Lowery immediately had his name taken by John Beaton for a clumsy foul on Nygren.

Despite their precarious position, to their credit, Kilmarnock didn’t come to Parkhead just to defend. Tyreece John-Jules, their main striker, had an abundance of support in the form of Owen, Greg Kiltie and Joe Hugill.

Between them, they fashioned enough half chances. They just didn’t have ice in the veins at the right moments.

Hogh was right to feel aggrieved at the decision which cost him that elusive goal. Truthfully, there was very little in Mika Baur’s challenge on Kiltie which was retrospectively penalised.

Duran gathered and fed Nygren. The Swede swivelled and rolled the ball into Hogh’s path. A crisp right-foot finish was not that of a player lacking in confidence.

The Dane’s lengthy celebrations included a look up to the heavens. By the time Beaton made his way to the monitor and chalked the goal off, Hogh could have been forgiven for feeling that someone up there had it in for him.

To Hogh’s credit, he didn’t let it affect him. Moments later, after being played in by Duran, he was only denied his revenge by the outstretched leg of Nico Mantl.

Hogh then played Duran in with a clever back-heel. The Colombian lashed his effort just wide.

Celtic’s pressure was mounting and they edged in front on 26 minutes with Duran, yet again excellent throughout, the architect.

His clipped cross from the right caught the Kilmarnock defence flat-footed. Scales extended his left foot and watched the ball rise to the point where it appeared to be going over.

With Mantl hoping that it would clear his bar, it looped into the net. There was no disputing this one.

Required to take more risks, Killie had to be careful when they turned the ball over. Hugill and Cathal McCarthy were both booked for crude fouls on Duran and Jota, respectively.

While Celtic began to control the affair, the visitors were not without their moments. Michael Schjonning-Larsen’s stinging strike from 25 yards forced Johnstone into another solid stop.

While Jota looked good in moments, it’s clearly going to take some time before he’s fully up to speed. The same might apply to Hyun-jun Yang, the man who replaced him at the interval, after being sidelined for six weeks with a hamstring injury.

Donovan came close to wrapping up the three points with an enterprising run and shot with his left foot which flew just wide.

Duran’s close control and speed across the ground bought him a yard for a shot. Having seen it late, Mantl dealt with it well.

Hogh actually performed well here. He was tidy with the ball and frequently brought team-mates into play. He just didn’t hit the target often enough. That again cost him when he spun neatly in the box and tried his luck midway through the second period.

You couldn’t fault him when he seized on Kieran Tierney’s cross, though. The saving block from Killie substitute MJ Kamson-Kamara was tremendous.

With the home supporters longing for a clinching second goal, Duran dumped a shot into the side netting before the industrious Baur was flagged offside after firing home.

The wait would go on. Initially adjudged to have struck Aaron Tshibola’s arm just outside the box, Yang’s strike would earn Celtic a spot-kick with 14 minutes remaining after a VAR review.

Nygren just never looked confident as he spotted the ball. Mantl dived to his right and made a comfortable save. Those hoping for a sharp exit sat back in their seats.

The sight of Baur hobbling badly after being caught by Tshibola didn’t make the viewing in the final moments any easier. All that truly counted for the home fans, though, was that Celtic got over the line.

#2362. Bernard Vellut : « On veut que ce soit une belle fête »

À quelques jours du choc face à l’AS Saint-Étienne au Parc des Sports d’Annecy, pour le compte du 7e tour de la Coupe de France, le GFA Rumilly Vallières est en pleine effervescence. Le club haut-savoyard cultive un modèle unique porté par quatre co-présidents et un esprit résolument familial. L'un d'eux, Bernard Vellut (à droite sur la photo), revient pour nous sur les coulisses de cette ascension fulgurante, les défis structurels du club et l'excitation qui entoure la venue des Verts.

Bonjour Bernard Vellut et merci de nous accorder ces quelques minutes d’interview. Le club de Rumilly Vallières a la particularité d'être dirigé par quatre co-présidents avec chacun un rôle dédié. En ce qui te concerne, tu as en charge les instances de football et les collectivités territoriales. Comment est née cette organisation assez unique et comment vous répartissez-vous la charge au quotidien ?

Effectivement, nous sommes quatre co-présidents. Comment cela est-il arrivé ? Moi j'étais président du club de Rumilly et Luc Chabert était président du club de Vallières, à trois kilomètres de chez nous. On a donc évoqué un rapprochement de nos deux clubs, plutôt que de se tirer dans les pattes, sans parler encore de fusion. Mais il y avait toujours quelque chose qui clochait au niveau de détails plus ou moins importants. C'est toujours une question humaine de toute façon.

À un moment donné, on s’est dit qu'il fallait arrêter de se piquer les joueurs et réfléchir à faire quelque chose de plus grand. Avec Luc Chabert, qui a une très grosse entreprise de fromagerie avec une dizaine de laiteries, on s’est dit : « On y va ! ». On s’est donc réunis une fois, deux fois, trois fois. Au début ça tiquait un petit peu, mais on est arrivés à se dire qu'on allait commencer par tout le cursus jeunes. Les jeunes, eux, c'est d’abord la couleur du maillot, les histoires de personnes ils n’en ont rien à cirer ! On a fait deux années comme ça avec les jeunes et ça a tellement bien marché qu'on a ensuite souhaité fusionner tout le club. De là est née la structure des quatre co-présidents : j'avais un vice-président avec moi et Luc Chabert avait le sien de son côté. On s’est dit qu’on n’allait pas faire d'histoires et qu'à quatre co-présidents, on ne serait pas trop pour gérer le club.

Les choses se sont déroulées de la meilleure des façons. Au-delà de la présidence, on est tous devenus amis, ainsi que nos épouses respectives. Quand tu as quatre co-présidents comme ça qui mènent la barque, tous les gens derrière voient que ça marche super bien et l'engouement est parti. Au niveau de la répartition : Bruno Piccon s'occupe de toute la partie jeunes, Luc Chabert gère les partenaires, François Baudet s'occupe de la communication et du matériel, et pour ma part, je m'occupe des instances, des terrains, de la mairie de Vallières, du district, de la Ligue et de la Fédération. Je fais également partie du comité directeur de la Ligue où je représente les clubs nationaux de la région Rhône-Alpes, ainsi qu'au siège à Paris.

En dehors de cela, on a tous notre boulot, bien sûr. Toutes les trois semaines, on se réunit et on mange ensemble pour échanger sur les perspectives, les actions en cours ou à venir, et ce qui a été bien ou moins bien fait. Derrière, on a un comité directeur pour décider des orientations dans les différentes commissions, et trois semaines après, ces commissions nous rendent compte de leurs résultats. On avance comme ça, sans compter les coups de téléphone et les messages tous les deux jours... On communique beaucoup ! On est obligés de travailler comme cela maintenant parce qu'on a quand même 800 licenciés, ce n'est pas rien.

Comme tu le disais, le club est né d'une fusion en 2018, et il se retrouve aujourd'hui à une très belle place en championnat (le club est actuellement 2e de sa poule en National 1, à 3 points de Nîmes avec un match de retard, ndp²). Le club a aussi connu une épopée fantastique en Coupe de France en 2021. Quel regard portez-vous sur cette ascension fulgurante et est-ce que le monde professionnel est une ambition ou un doux rêve ?

On a l'habitude de faire les choses les unes après les autres. Quand on est montés, on voulait voir à quel niveau on pouvait se situer. Aujourd'hui, il ne faut pas se leurrer, les joueurs comme le staff sont pratiquement tous professionnels. Comme nous avons un budget serré, nous souhaitons avancer pas à pas et ne surtout pas brûler les étapes.

Au niveau du coach, nous avons un gars super, Cédric Rullier, qui a essentiellement recruté régionalement. On ne voulait pas recruter trop loin parce que la vie est très chère en Haute-Savoie et qu'il faut se loger. Maintenant qu'on a un niveau honnête avec de l’ambition, on va chercher un petit peu plus loin, mais toujours des joueurs ciblés sur leur mentalité et leur respect. C'est après les qualités sportives bien sûr, mais un joueur qui ne rentre pas dans notre moule mentalement, on ne le prend pas. On veut garder notre esprit familial, même si on s'est beaucoup professionnalisés.

On a énormément de bénévoles et on veut que la mayonnaise prenne entre eux et les joueurs. C'est ce qui fait notre force aujourd'hui : le groupe vit très bien, l'entraîneur est exceptionnel et les bénévoles sont hyper dévoués. Dès que tu demandes quelque chose, ils sont tous derrière toi pour t'aider. Aujourd'hui, c'est fantastique !

La Coupe de France, cela n’a rien à voir avec le championnat, les émotions sont multipliées par dix ou quinze. En 2021, lors de notre exploit, on a commencé en gagnant aux tirs au but contre un club de district, un truc de dingue. Petit à petit, on a passé les tours : on a sorti Prix-lès-Mézières et le FC Annecy aux tirs au but, puis Le Puy en huitièmes, Toulouse en quarts, avant de tomber contre Monaco en demi-finale. C'était complètement fou.

Le seul bémol, c'est que ça s'est joué pendant le Covid, donc il n'y avait pas de supporters au stade. Mais ça ne nous a pas empêchés de nous retrouver le soir des victoires. La mairie et la gendarmerie ont été très sympas avec nous parce qu'ils savaient qu'ils ne pouvaient pas faire autrement. J'ai une petite anecdote pour le match contre Monaco : le préfet avait donné l'autorisation à deux ou trois équipes de jeunes de nous accompagner à la sortie du restaurant à midi. Sauf qu'on s'est retrouvés à 3 000 dans les rues ! Les gendarmes n'ont rien pu faire, c'était un truc de fou. C'est une expérience qu'on ne reverra peut-être jamais, en tout cas pas moi, mais c'est tout le mal que je souhaite au club.

Depuis, ça fait trois ans qu'on se fait éliminer aux tirs au but. On s'était dit que cette année, il fallait que ça change pour aller chercher un 32e ou un gros club. On n'est pas encore en 32e, on a eu quelques soucis et on est encore passés aux tirs au but au tour précédent. J'ai l'impression que c'est dans la tête : quand tu rencontres une équipe plus faible, tu te mets à son diapason. Mais avec les joueurs qu'on a aujourd'hui, si on tombe contre une équipe plus forte, ils savent hausser leur niveau. On ne lâche rien. Par exemple, hier à Saint-Priest, il y avait 2-2 à un quart d'heure de la fin. On fait rentrer le banc, pouf, on met 4-2, et si le match continue on en met encore deux ou trois. C'est formidable.

Parlons un peu des infrastructures du club et de vos perspectives d'évolution. De quels terrains disposez-vous actuellement ?

On a un terrain en herbe à Rumilly avec un synthétique à côté. Le centre de Vallières est consacré aux jeunes et aux matchs de Ligue pour nos réserves (l'équipe 2 joue en R1 et l'équipe 3 en R3). Là-bas, on a un terrain en herbe et un synthétique tout neuf, deux jolis terrains.

Là où le bât blesse aujourd'hui, c'est à Rumilly : notre synthétique doit être refait et on voudrait améliorer le terrain en herbe. En ce moment il est beau parce qu'il pleut, mais l'été il est sec et l'hiver il gèle. Ce n'est pas une vraie pelouse de stade, elle a 50 ans. Les jardiniers s'en occupent très bien, mais il faut refaire la surface. On hésite entre synthétique, hybride ou herbe. On se bat un peu avec la mairie et la communauté de communes pour avoir une vision sur les prochaines années. Un beau terrain amène du beau jeu et des spectateurs, c'est un tout. Les municipalités ne sont pas contre, mais il faut trouver les fonds et les subventions.

Vous pouvez bénéficier d'aides financières des collectivités ?

Oui, la mairie nous donne 100 000 euros et la communauté de communes 100 000 euros aussi. Pour le département, les subventions dépendent de la division dans laquelle tu évolues. Avec la réforme des championnats et les changements d'appellation, on est en train de regarder à quoi on a droit en tant que premier niveau amateur de France. C'est un combat de tous les jours. Il faut rien lâcher, tout en gardant un bon état d'esprit et en évitant les conflits. Ça a déjà bien avancé par rapport à nos débuts, mais il en faut encore plus.

Quel est votre budget actuel et est-il stable ?

On est à 1,9 million d'euros. Quand on a fusionné, on était à 600 000 euros. Aujourd'hui, on doit avoir le dixième budget de notre poule. On est dans une poule Sud très relevée avec des clubs comme Nîmes ou Toulon. C'est quasiment une petite division supérieure, capable de rivaliser avec le milieu de tableau au-dessus.

Il y a aussi quatre clubs de la région lyonnaise dans votre groupe. Comment vous positionnez-vous face à eux, notamment sur le recrutement ?

Ils ont un gros avantage sur nous : ils ont des pépinières de joueurs partout, avec 20 ou 25 clubs au kilomètre carré. Nous, à part Annecy avec qui on travaille en partenariat, on est assez isolés. Lyon est à 120 kilomètres. Et puis à Rumilly, on est à côté d'Annecy et de Genève : un T2 coûte 800 ou 900 euros par mois, ce n'est pas 300 euros comme en Lozère ou dans le Centre. C'est pour ça qu'on fait très attention à qui on fait venir.

Justement, comment êtes-vous organisés pour recruter sans cellule de recrutement professionnelle ?

Nos deux coachs travaillent énormément à la vidéo et vont voir beaucoup de matchs. On a aussi un directeur sportif à plein temps et une petite cellule composée d'anciens joueurs du club. On les envoie observer des matchs à Mâcon, Bourg ou Thonon avec des fiches précises sur un joueur ciblé. On travaille aussi avec quelques agents qu'on connaît bien, même si ce n'est pas notre tasse de thé parce qu'un agent ne te dira jamais que son joueur est mauvais.

Avant de recruter un joueur, il est visionné trois ou quatre fois, en vidéo et en physique. Ensuite, le directeur sportif l'appelle pour sonder sa mentalité, et celle de sa compagne ou de sa famille, car c'est très important. On leur explique le coût de la vie ici. Niveau infrastructures, on est équipés avec salle de muscu et bains froids, mais on n'a pas des salaires de 4 000 euros comme dans le Midi. On ne veut pas qu'un gars arrive et dise qu'il ne peut pas vivre ici. Après, on se voit, on discute beaucoup avec Luc Chabert, le directeur sportif et les coachs. On marche au feeling, et en discutant avec la maman ou la copine, on voit vite si on ne se trompe pas sur la mentalité. En moyenne, entre la visio, les appels et les repas tous ensemble, on se voit plusieurs fois avant de signer. On prépare déjà la saison prochaine, en regardant plutôt vers le nord que vers le sud, car un joueur qui habite à Nîmes ou Hyères au bord de la mer avec un bon salaire est très dur à faire venir en Haute-Savoie.

Vous faites aussi monter des jeunes de votre réserve, j'imagine ?

Oui, mais on part de loin. Quand on a lancé la fusion chez les jeunes en 2016, le niveau n'était pas très élevé. Aujourd'hui, toutes nos équipes de U15, U17 et U18 évoluent en Régional 1. Notre équipe réserve, qui joue aussi en R1, est composée à 60 % de jeunes du club. Ils ne sont pas encore prêts pour briguer une place de titulaire en équipe première, mais chaque année, le coach en intègre quelques-uns. Cette saison, trois jeunes du club font tous les entraînements avec le groupe, et un ou deux sont vraiment prometteurs parce qu'ils haussent leur niveau.

C'est très dur d'atteindre le niveau national quand des gamins n'ont pas eu une grosse formation dès l'âge de 7 ou 8 ans. Maintenant, on commence à récolter les fruits de notre travail : ceux qu'on a pris à 7 ou 8 ans en ont 13 ou 14 aujourd'hui, et on voit qu'il y a un vrai niveau. Pour nous, faire monter des jeunes du club, c'est aussi l'avantage de ne pas avoir à leur chercher un appartement ! Mais en attendant, on recrute surtout des joueurs qui ont un bon niveau en National 2 et qui ont faim, plutôt que d'aller chercher des gars de Ligue 2 pour qui venir jouer chez nous serait vécu comme un échec.

Tu disais que les joueurs fonctionnent comme des pros. Ils vivent tous du football aujourd'hui ?

Statutairement on est amateurs, mais dans le fonctionnement, on est comme des pros. À part deux ou trois jeunes en service civique qui montent dans le groupe, tous les joueurs vivent de ça. Ils s'entraînent une à deux fois par jour. Comme les matchs ont souvent lieu le vendredi soir et qu'il faut partir dès le jeudi midi pour aller à Châteauroux ou ailleurs, il est impossible qu'ils travaillent à côté.

Sans entrer dans les détails indiscrets, quel est l'ordre d'idée des rémunérations ?

Je ne vais pas donner de chiffres précis. On fait des contrats d'un an, et on a deux ou trois joueurs qui sortent un peu du lot. Mais globalement, nos salaires sont 30 à 40 % moins élevés que dans les clubs du Midi. Ils en vivent, mais ce ne sont pas des salaires de cadres. Après, il n'y a pas que l'argent : les joueurs connaissent les conditions et le cadre de vie avant de signer. S'ils acceptent, ils savent où ils mettent les pieds et ça se passe très bien.

Un mot sur le vestiaire et le staff : vous avez notamment Cyril Martin-Pichon, formé à l'ASSE et qui a beaucoup joué dans les équipes de jeunes et même en réserve, qui est un cadre chez vous, et un staff très fidèle au club.

Oui, Cyril Martin-Pichon a quitté le système pro parce qu'il n'y adhérait plus tellement. Il pense au collectif et au plaisir avant tout. Ça fait trois ans qu'il est chez nous, c'est un excellent joueur avec une mentalité irréprochable, un vrai cadre.

Pour le staff, notre entraîneur Cédric Rullier est passé par Annecy, Chambéry et Lyon. Ça fait trois ou quatre ans qu'il est là. Il est diététicien à l'hôpital de Chambéry et, depuis l'an dernier, il a mis son métier entre parenthèses pour se consacrer uniquement au club. Tout le reste du staff est issu du club : son adjoint Damien Guerrier, notre jeune entraîneur des gardiens qu'on a formé, et nos deux préparateurs physiques. Ce ne sont pas des gens arrivés sur le tard, on les connaît depuis dix ans, depuis tout petits. Ils sont rémunérés par le club, sont exigeants — on améliore nos équipements chaque année — mais ils sont surtout dévoués à 100 %.

J'ai été impressionné par les statistiques de Rumilly cette année : vous avez une puissance de feu phénoménale en attaque ! Le nombre de buts que vous marquez est impressionnant.

C'est bizarre parce qu'en début de saison, on se disait justement qu'on était un peu courts en attaque ! Et finalement, on marque beaucoup de buts, je crois qu'on a la meilleure attaque de toutes les poules confondues. C'est grâce à notre force mentale et au coach qui tient bien son groupe. Les joueurs savent que si ça ne va pas, ils peuvent partir, mais à côté de ça, ils ont un cadre hyper convivial avec les bénévoles qui sont aux petits soins pour eux.

Il y a des joueurs qui se révèlent, comme Fortier qui avait dû marquer trois buts l'an dernier et qui est dans tous les bons coups cette saison. Et puis on a des gars très techniques au milieu comme Cyril Martin-Pichon, Yoann Martelat ou Quentin Foulay, qui donnent d'excellents ballons devant. J'espère que ça va durer.

Dans votre championnat, il n'y a que le premier qui monte, ou le deuxième joue des barrages ?

Il n'y a que le premier qui monte, il n'y a pas de barrages pour le deuxième. Après, comme chaque année avec les passages devant la DNCG, il arrive parfois que des places se libèrent et que le meilleur deuxième des trois poules soit repêché.

Et la Ligue 3 professionnelle, si l'occasion se présente, vous y pensez ?

Comme je le disais au départ, ce sont les résultats qui décideront. L'an dernier déjà, à la trêve, on était premiers et on commençait à se poser la question. Sportivement, on peut y arriver si les joueurs le méritent. Mais aujourd'hui, je trouve que la Ligue 3 n'est pas encore prête au niveau de la Fédération, et les aides financières ne sont pas forcément à la hauteur de ce qui avait été annoncé.

Un bon copain qui est au Puy m'a prévenu que le cahier des charges est énorme : il faut des caméras, des parcages visiteurs, un directeur de club. Si tu ne remplis pas tous les critères, on te retient des pénalités sur les subventions, et sur les 300 000 euros prévus, il ne t'en reste plus que 150 000 à l'arrivée. Les déplacements ne seraient pas un problème puisqu'on traverse déjà toute la France, mais il faudrait vraiment de grosses aides du département, de la mairie et de la communauté de communes pour mettre le stade aux normes.

Revenons à la Coupe de France. Quelle a été la première réaction au club quand le tirage au sort a désigné l'AS Saint-Étienne ?

Moi, j'étais à Paris pour le tirage. Trois journalistes m'ont sauté dessus avant même la fin pour me demander ce que j'en pensais et où on allait jouer ! Ce n'est pas un soulagement, c'est l'opportunité qu'on cherchait : tomber sur un club pro dès le 7e tour.

La question du stade s'est tout de suite posée parce qu'à Rumilly, on a une capacité de 2 500 places, impossible d'accueillir les Verts. Sébastien Faraglia, le président du FC Annecy, m'a tout de suite appelé pour nous dépanner. C'est un ami, je l'ai connu joueur et entraîneur à Rumilly, et Luc Chabert est aussi partenaire du FC Annecy avec ses entreprises. Il y a une vraie osmose.

Au club, ça a été l'embrasement général, tout le monde ne parlait que de ça alors qu'on avait deux matchs de championnat à jouer entre-temps. L'entraîneur a très bien géré le coup. Dès le lundi après le tirage, il a enfermé les joueurs dans le vestiaire et leur a dit : « Le premier qui parle de Saint-Étienne ou qui s'économise, avec moi ça ne marchera pas ». Résultat, on a gagné 5-0 contre Istres et 4-2 hier. Maintenant, on peut se consacrer à Saint-Étienne.

Comment abordez-vous ce match face à un monument du foot français ?

Il y a deux divisions d'écart, ce sont des pros, et Saint-Étienne est un club que tout le monde adore. Après, c'est la trêve internationale, donc ils ont pas mal de joueurs partis en sélection. Il restera une très belle équipe, on ne va pas se cacher, mais ce ne sera pas l'équipe type. En coupe, les niveaux se resserrent et les équipes inférieures haussent souvent le ton. D'habitude, on dit que le terrain avantage le petit, mais là on jouera sur la pelouse hybride d'Annecy, l'une des meilleures de France. Au niveau technique et du jeu, ça va vraiment être le top.

Le Parc des Sports d'Annecy sera à guichets fermés ?

Pas encore. Saint-Étienne a eu une ouverture prioritaire pour ses Ultras — il y aura d'ailleurs deux groupes d'Ultras de Saint Etienne qui feront le déplacement. La billetterie grand public a ouvert depuis avant-hier. Le stade fait 13 000 places, mais la jauge de sécurité sera fixée autour de 10 000 ou 10 500 spectateurs. Pour des raisons de sécurité, les spectateurs en tribune ne peuvent pas croiser le parcage stéphanois pour aller aux toilettes, donc on doit condamner un quart de virage, ce qui enlève environ 2 000 places.

On a vendu entre 5 000 et 6 500 places les deux ou trois premiers jours, et là on tourne à 100 ou 150 billets par jour. S'il fait beau dimanche, tous les papas avec leurs enfants vont se dire qu'ils prennent une place à 10 euros pour voir le match. S'il pleut, ils viendront moins. Ils annoncent un petit peu de pluie, on verra bien.

Vous sentez toute la région derrière vous ?

Oui, on sent que les Verts arrivent à Annecy. Bon, les Annéciens sont un peu habitués puisqu'ils les jouent en championnat. Ici, les gens ne parlent même pas de notre équipe, ils parlent des Verts qui débarquent ! C'est un club emblématique, historique, c'est comme ça. Quant à nos joueurs, je les ai vus hier soir : ils sont confiants, dans leur bulle. Ils pensent à Saint-Étienne mais n'en font pas une maladie, pour ne pas jouer le match trois fois dans leur tête avant dimanche. Mais à partir d'aujourd'hui, ça va forcément commencer à trotter.

Si tu devais signer tout de suite pour un scénario de match — même une qualification stéphanoise à l'arraché après une rencontre dantesque — qu'est-ce qui te ferait dire que l'événement est une réussite totale ?

L'événement sera une réussite à partir du moment où le stade sera plein. Enfin, il sera plein, j’en suis sûr ! Ensuite, il faut qu'on fasse un match à la hauteur de notre niveau. Même si on perd, si c'est un beau match, les gens seront contents : ils auront vu une belle équipe de Saint-Étienne et ils auront vu qu'on a su répondre présent. Ce sera une fête réussie, en espérant derrière que ça nous laisse quand même un petit reliquat financier. On aura fait notre boulot face aux Verts.

Saint-Étienne devrait vous laisser sa part de la recette, normalement ?

On verra. Je pense que si on accueille bien les spectateurs, si on fait une belle fête, si les supporters — parce que ce sont eux qui font l'ambiance — sont bien reçus et qu'il n'y a pas de problème avec l'équipe de Saint-Étienne, ils la laisseront. Ils nous ont demandé plein de choses au niveau des vestiaires, mais si tout se passe bien, il n'y aura pas de souci. De toute façon, ce n'est pas dans notre habitude de recevoir des équipes en se disant qu'on va les matraquer. Quand on a reçu Monaco, ils avaient fait notre éloge pour l'accueil, parce qu'on n'avait pas joué pour blesser ou pour détruire. Ce sera exactement dans la même lignée : on n'est pas là pour casser du Vert, loin de là, au contraire, on est là pour profiter. Par contre, si on a l'occasion de passer, on passera, c'est sûr !

Tu parlais des demandes de Saint-Étienne pour les vestiaires, ils ont beaucoup d'exigences ?

Ça ne m'a pas trop surpris. Comme notre coach est diététicien, quand on se déplace, on a déjà pas mal de choses : différentes eaux, des minéraux, des barres de chocolat, des casse-croûtes, des tapis, des biscuits... Là, c'est dans le même style, en amplifié : de l'eau, des barres de céréales, des fruits, etc. C'est assez énorme, mais ça ne nous surprend pas parce qu'on fait déjà un peu la même chose.

Qui sont vos interlocuteurs au sein du club de Saint-Étienne pour organiser tout ça ?

Quand j'étais à Paris pour le tirage, un ancien joueur de Saint-Étienne qui fait partie de l'encadrement m'a contacté en me disant que le directeur allait m'appeler, et cinq minutes après, il m'appelait. On est en contact avec le directeur du club (Jean-François Soucasse, NDLR) et le directeur de la sécurité. Les relations ont été très sympathiques avec des gens très gentils. Nous avons de très bons rapports.

Une dernière question : est-ce que tu serais d’accord pour que l'année prochaine,  peut-être à la même époque, on refasse un petit point pour voir un peu où vous en êtes un an après avoir joué les Verts ?

Oui, il n'y a pas de souci. Ce sera avec plaisir.

Eh bien écoute Bernard, un grand merci pour ton temps, bonne journée et bon match pour dimanche !

#2360. Freiburg duo discuss national team life ahead of Germany's Serbia test

Germany’s final international break of 2026 sees Jürgen Klopp’s charges visit Serbia tonight before hosting the Netherlands on Monday. Sitting on five points after four outings, this year’s UEFA Nations League campaign hangs in the balance and even a positive result in Belgrade will only force an all-important decider.

Trailing both the Netherlands and Greece by three points, Germany are in desperate need of a win against Serbia in tonight’s encounter ahead of their final match at the Olympiastadion.

SC Freiburg’s Philipp Treu and Derry Scherhant took up media duties, with the former touching on his development while the latter discussed his 24th birthday celebrations with the national team just three days ago.

On initially being welcomed into the fold, Treu was full of praise for familiar faces, singling out Nico Schlotterbeck in particular: “One thing that really helped me at the start was already knowing quite a few of the players.”

As one of the relatively new additions, Treu also faced a dilemma on what to do with his debut shirt: “It caused quite a stir, even at home (laughs). My girlfriend and I have agreed that it can go on the rail at home where I keep my most special shirts.”

In terms of matters on the pitch, attention then turned to the full-back’s playing style: “Moving to SC Freiburg has really helped me because I’m getting more opportunities to get involved in the final third. That’s been hugely beneficial in terms of improving my attacking play.”

Determined to provide an outlet on the flank, Treu expressed his desire to make a difference during this camp: “I want to offer even more of a goal threat in the next two games.”

With the Serbian federation currently being punished for a number of discriminatory incidents, tonight’smatch will have a slightly different feel. On playing behind closed doors, Treu naturally expressed his disappointment: “It’s obviously a shame because every footballer wants to play in front of fans.”

Drawing on his experience from the pandemic and trying to see the positive side, he reassuringly added: “One possible advantage is that we’ll be able to communicate even better on the pitch. But overall, the circumstances won’t make much difference to us.”

Scherhant, who also earned his first international call-up in September, celebrated another important milestone with the team on Tuesday:

“The team sang for me, in several different languages too.”

Adopting a slightly more serious tone, he added: “But I’ve already received the best birthday present I could ask for, which is being here with the national team.”

Despite the task at hand in Belgrade, the young forward was asked about potentially representing Germany at the Olympiastadion on Monday, offering up this very sincere response: “That would be an incredibly special moment for me. It’s almost impossible to imagine. I grew up just five minutes from the stadium.”

With confidence and maturity, Scherhant also showed a willingness to adapt his position in service of the whole: “We’re encouraged to stay high and wide and have the confidence to take defenders on. That’s what I tried to do in my first two games and it gave me another boost in confidence. I want to keepbuilding on that.”

#2359. Man United 4-4 Aston Villa PLAYER RATINGS: Who looks like the only ray of light at Old Trafford? Whose display was a mess? And which 3/10 star was absolutely hopeless?

Published: 13:52 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 15:31 EST, 8 November 2026

Manchester United will rot 18th in the Premier League table during the international break even after scrambling an unlikely 4-4 draw against Aston Villa.

Michael Carrick's side fought back from a two-goal deficit, inflicted in just four first-half minutes, and went into the interval level after goals from Bruno Fernandes and Youri Tielemans.

They had to pull off the trick a second time after John McGinn's penalty and Leon Goretzka's back-post tap-in made it 4-2 to Unai Emery's visitors.

But Carrick's side refused to throw in the towel despite how hopeless they were for the majority of this game and his substitutes paid off handsomely.

Joshua Zirkzee made it 4-3 in the 92nd minute, teed up by fellow sub Patrick Dorgu, and Dorgu completed the comeback with a 96th-minute finish of his own to send Old Trafford into raptures.

CEO Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox celebrated in the directors' box, all while Emery stormed off down the tunnel before the final whistle.

Daily Mail Sport's NATHAN SALT was in position at Old Trafford to grade both sets of players...

Patrick Dorgu (right) salvaged the most unlikely of points for Manchester United on Sunday

John McGinn (right) was immense but Villa suffered when he went off as they finished 4-4

A mess of a performance. The Belgian got totally muddled up when he tried to save Alysson's first-minute shot with his knees and it flew between his legs. He could then only parry Alysson's shot straight to Jackson, who made it 2-0. Sold short by his defenders for goals three and four but the United malaise seems to have worn down Lammens, who looked so good last season.

Looked like a rabbit in headlights at the start of the game as the ball ricocheted off him en route to Alysson making it 1-0. Did get an assist for cutting the ball back to Tielemans just before half-time but as an overall performance, Villa feasted on his shortcomings.

Looked like the only United defender capable of defending to a respectable level until even he got sucked into the mistake-fest that United put on at the back. A positive first half, in which he made a strong recovery run to deny Jackson racing through for a third goal, was undone when he inexplicably headed onto his own bar under no pressure to gift Villa a fourth as Leon Goretzka turned in the knockdown.

Made a key last-man challenge but otherwise this was another game where he hasn't been able to organise a defence that is crying out for leadership. Forced off at half-time just days after appearing to tweak his hamstring against Roma.

Absolutely hopeless, just as he was against Roma on Tuesday. Switched off in the first minute and made a weak attempt to block Alysson, who scored. Then, two minutes into the second half, he recklessly fouled Alysson to give away a penalty. Many fans cheered when he was substituted. There has to be a better left back solution than Dalot right now.

Thousands of United fans cheered at the announcement that Diogo Dalot was going off

Scored his first goal for United, and against his former side no less. When United were rolling, Tielemans was at the heart of it all. Faded in the second half when Villa took the lead again and he was unsurprisingly taken off as Carrick's first substitution.

Neat and tidy on the ball but got bested by McGinn for the time the Scottish was on the pitch. One of the few who genuinely does look to play forwards when possible.

His goal drought goes on as we rapidly approach a year since his last one. He's electric when he's bearing down on a full-back and he had to score when played in by Tielemans after escaping the sight of Maatsen. Does so much well but his end product simply must improve or he won't merit starts.

Scored a penalty for the second time in a week but cut a frustrated figure for large spells here as the team collapsed. His corner onto the head of Dorgu was the type of quality you need in crunch moments and that's why he rarely comes off.

Looks like the only ray of light these days as this feckless United side fall to even greater depths. Did well to wrestle himself free of Harwood-Bellis to break and win a penalty after a clueless foul from Boubacar Kamara. Stung the palms of Suzuki soon after.

Matheus Cunha dragged United back into the game and did brilliantly to win an early penalty

Didn't do enough to trouble Villa's backline as he once again found himself deployed down the middle. Only took three shots in this basketball-type game.

Could only scratch his chin in disbelief 60 seconds in when Villa took the lead and he looked down and out when it became 4-2 early in the second half. But he was proactive with his substitutions and they all paid off. He deserves credit for that.

Booked for a reckless - and late - lunge. Interesting he got the nod ahead of Matthijs de Ligt at half-time.

Goal and assist off the bench. Has to be the starter at left back from here on out or United will remain in a washing machine cycle of misery defensively.

Drew a furious response when he ballooned a shot over the bar when United were chasing the game late on.

Electric, again. He's fearless and United have needed that this week when the chips were down.

Joshua Zirkzee (for Bryan Mbeumo, 83) - 7

Clinical when the chance came and it was his goal at 4-3 that gave United the opportunity to stage a dramatic late finish. He gives them a focal point that they are crying out for.

Conceded four times and was livid at the end that VAR didn't punish Lammens backing in to him with the last action of the game. It was a performance that saw Suzuki make some huge saves in the first half. There was one big right hand to deny Amad just before half-time and he was also full stretch to tip Cunha's curling shot away, too, just before. Couldn't see Tielemans' equaliser at 2-2 with Mbeumo in front and will feel aggrieved at the finale.

First Premier League start for Villa since his £30million summer switch and he will have not expected for it to come at right back. Found himself in an unfortunate mismatch against Cunha and the ease at which the Brazilian brushed him off shows why it's unlikely to be a long-term assignment given the quality of wide players in the league.

Was so often given the mocking treatment that has now fallen onto the likes of Dalot and Mazraoui during his time at Manchester United but Lindelof is a better player than many here care to admit. Reads the game well and did well to cut out a run into the box by Patrick Dorgu ten minutes from time but was found wanting on the final set piece that triggered the equaliser.

Victor Lindelof is a better player than a lot of United fans may care to admit

Felt like a quarterback for this Villa side for most of it with how many touches he was able to take under very little pressure. Had a big smile on his face for long spells but that was firmly washed off by the end. Abysmal marking as the game entered stoppage time and both Zirkzee and Dorgu scored.

Was given a torrid time by Amad in the first half and still gives opponents too many opportunities to get at him. Shea Lacey had his way with him when he came on, too. Defensively not good enough.

Spent years getting linked to Manchester United but now here as an opposition player, he made them pay with a goal at the Stretford End to make it 4-2. Tidy player.

Idiotic foul to bring Cunha down to give United a lifeline in a game they weren't in 15 minutes in. Seemed to be caught in two minds whether to push onto Tielemans or Mainoo in the 30-minute spell where United looked like a competent football team. Breathed a sigh of relief when the hosts resorted to type.

A total menace that knew within seconds that he had the beating of Dalot each and every time. Clinical strike in the opening minute to give Villa the lead and his trickery and directness paid off a second time at the start of the second half when he baited Dalot into conceding a penalty. Brilliant outing.

Alysson won a penalty after outwitting Diogo Dalot in the United box early in the second half

Best player on the pitch. He made the right decision time after time and even when pressed on the ball when United started to find a groove, he looked to have unshakeable composure. Cool as you like to score a penalty in front of the Stretford End – not least having missed a penalty against Fulham – and then it was his free-kick that led to the fourth. Still feels underrated.

A fearless dribbler that has the potential to be the jewel of this Villa crown in the years to come. In a league that has long ground a lot of flair players down, his physical dribbling through the lines is a sight to behold. Completed more dribbles than any other player by the time he came off.

Quick as a flash to score from Lammens' spill four minutes in and really should have done better when he was played through one v one, only to see Yoro beat him for pace. Led the line in a way that Manchester United were crying out for.

Has never won at Old Trafford as Villa boss and this is the most regrettable of the lot having led both 2-0 and 4-2 here, only to throw it away in both halves. He stormed off furiously before the final whistle.

#2357. Garby oh Garby

Valeureux capitaine d'une équipe de Rumilly-Vallières quasiment au complet pour affronter les Verts (seul le défenseur central Marc Laurent, blessé à la cheville depuis le début de saison, est indisponible), il cause, cause, Garby (Nicolas de son prénom) !

Agé de 34 ans, le latéral gauche s'est en effet confié au Dauphiné Libéré avant de retrouver dimanche un Parc des Sports d'Annecy qu'il connait bien pour avoir défendu les couleurs des Annéciens pendant 12 ans et pour y avoir vécu 5 montées ! Extraits.

"Ça a été un peu dur de quitter Annecy car c’était un rêve de devenir pro. Mais au GFA Rumilly-Vallières, je me suis tout de suite senti super à l’aise. Le niveau n’a pas été un problème car je n’oublie pas que j’avais commencé à Annecy en R2. Je suis le seul joueur à travailler à côté du foot en tant que commercial chez TeamWork, à mi-temps. J’ai mon équilibre comme ça, je ne pouvais pas trouver mieux. 12 ans au FC Annecy, autant chez mon employeur et une cinquième saison en cours au GFA... J’aime les projets. L’histoire est belle aussi ici, ça se construit. Le GFA m’a fait me sentir comme un cadre. Ça m’a fait me sentir plus fort, plus légitime, m’a donné plus d’assurance. Avec l’âge, quand le corps va bien, on progresse."

#2356. Johan Cruyff’s grandson called up to play for USA under-18 team

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

The most famous name in Dutch football could appear on an international men’s team sheet for the first time in 30 years after Johan Cruyff’s grandson was called up for the United States’ upcoming matches in Europe.

Shae Cruijff-Beane, the son of Johan Cruyff’s daughter Chantal and her American husband Todd Beane, was included in the squad that will play against Norway and Portugal in the next seven days.

Cruijff-Beane plays as a midfielder for Gimnàstic de Tarragona, a club in the Spanish third tier based near Barcelona. His older brother Jordan also came through the club’s ranks and is currently playing US college football at Westmont College in California.

Shae, 17, is eligible to play for the USA through his father and Spain and the Netherlands through his mother, who spent part of her childhood in Catalonia while Johan Cruyff was playing for Barcelona in the 1970s.

The USA under-18 squad also includes an Ajax player, midfielder Xander Newstead, who grew up in San Francisco and joined the Amsterdam club’s youth team set-up at the age of 10, beforec signing professional terms last year.

Johan Cruyff went on to play in the United States for Los Angeles Aztecs and Washington Diplomats before returning to the Netherlands, winding down his career at Ajax and Feyenoord.

His son Jordi played for Barcelona and Manchester United and won nine international caps in 1996. Last year he was appointed technical director at Ajax, the club where his father began his career.
